tests/test_auth.py::test_login_page_has_a_document_title (0s) · no screenshot captured
driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

tests/test_error_handling.py::test_reset_password_empty_token_param_shows_invalid_link (0s) · no screenshot captured
driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any

tests/test_error_handling.py::test_reset_password_very_long_token_treated_as_present (0s) · no screenshot captured
driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

    def test_help_center_category_filter_narrows_results(authenticated_driver, base_url):
        # Help/index.tsx: category is independent state from the search query
        # (both filters AND together) -- selecting "Troubleshooting" narrows
        # results without touching the search box, avoiding the unreliable
        # Selenium .clear()-on-a-React-controlled-input interaction a
        # search-then-clear flow would need.
        #
        # Each card renders inside its own <motion.div> with a staggered
        # entrance animation (transition={{delay: i * 0.03}}) -- reading
        # .text immediately after a navigation/click can catch later cards
        # mid-fade, so wait_articles_settled polls until the count is stable
        # rather than reading it once.
        help_center = HelpCenterPage(authenticated_driver, base_url)

    def check_response(self, response: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        """Check that a JSON response from the WebDriver does not have an error.
    
        Args:
            response: The JSON response from the WebDriver server as a dictionary
                object.
    
        Raises:
            WebDriverException: If the response contains an error message.
        """
        status = response.get("status", None)
        if not status or status == ErrorCode.SUCCESS:
            return
        value = None
        message = response.get("message", "")
        screen: str = response.get("screen", "")

driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

tests/test_keyboard_navigation.py::test_forgot_password_form_reachable_via_tab (0s) · no screenshot captured
driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

    def check_response(self, response: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        """Check that a JSON response from the WebDriver does not have an error.
    
        Args:
            response: The JSON response from the WebDriver server as a dictionary
                object.
    
        Raises:
            WebDriverException: If the response contains an error message.
        """

        if not status or status == ErrorCode.SUCCESS:
            return

tests/test_password_recovery.py::test_forgot_password_page_renders (0s) · no screenshot captured
driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's

        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.

driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

tests/test_session_guards.py::test_authguard_redirects_unauthenticated_user_to_login[/dashboard] (0s) · no screenshot captured
driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

tests/test_session_guards.py::test_authguard_redirects_unauthenticated_user_to_login[/help] (0s) · no screenshot captured
driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

tests/test_session_guards.py::test_unguarded_route_reachable_without_login[/] (0s) · no screenshot captured
driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)

tests/test_shared_result.py::test_shared_result_page_requires_no_login (0s) · no screenshot captured
driver = <selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver (session="60eddc31fbd4f899e76cbbb13117cbc9")>
base_url = 'http://localhost:5173'

    @pytest.fixture
    def unauthenticated_driver(driver, base_url):
        """For tests that need a guest (logged-out) view of a GuestGuard-protected
        page (/signup, /login, /forgot-password, /reset-password) or that assert
        AuthGuard's redirect-to-/login behavior. Clears the shared driver's
        session cookies rather than assuming the browser starts each test
        logged out — it won't, once any test in this run has used
        authenticated_driver.
    
        Restores those cookies on teardown instead of leaving the browser
        logged out — confirmed necessary by a real full-suite run: without this,
        every authenticated_driver test that happened to run after ANY
        unauthenticated_driver test in collection order silently got a
        logged-out browser (authenticated_driver's fixture body only runs once
        per session and just returns the cached driver reference — it doesn't
        re-check auth state), got redirected to /login by AuthGuard, and timed
        out waiting for content that could never appear. Restoring via saved
        cookies rather than a real re-login keeps the same rate-limit
        protection authenticated_driver exists for.
    
        get_cookies()/delete_all_cookies() only see cookies visible from the
        *current page's path*, and this backend deliberately scopes its
        refresh_token cookie to path=/api/v1/auth (api/auth/service.py's
        REFRESH_COOKIE_PATH) — a path the frontend SPA itself never navigates to.
        Calling those from an ordinary frontend route therefore never actually
        deleted refresh_token; it stayed live in the browser, and the frontend
        HTTP client's own 401 -> silent-refresh-via-POST-/api/v1/auth/refresh
        flow used it to transparently re-authenticate mid-test the moment any
        guest-page aut… (truncated)
